# Joint SIL audit: examined difficult cases

**3,227 examined records were held. No new assignment was saved for these records.** This audit is partial: 29,525 other initially unlinked records have not yet been researched.

[Joint status](/Users/aryamanarora/Documents/Code/jambu-all/data/curation/etymology-lab/indo-aryan-sil-20260914/REVIEW.md) · [Every held record, exact candidates and evidence](/Users/aryamanarora/Documents/Code/jambu-all/data/curation/etymology-lab/indo-aryan-sil-20260914/audit-records.json) · [CSV with IDs and source locations](/Users/aryamanarora/Documents/Code/jambu-all/data/curation/etymology-lab/indo-aryan-sil-20260914/audit-records.csv)

## Issues requiring scholarly decisions

The groups below are ordered by affected-record count. They preserve possible loan transmission, competing branches, morphology and transcription concerns separately. Existing older links elsewhere in Jambu were discovery aids and were not re-audited by this pass.

### 5. The homepage links some kahā̃ forms to iha here, but CDIAL 1605 itself does not establish this interrogative derivation. CDIAL 3384 discusses kuha versus kva for where, while Platts p. 868 proposes kim + sthāna for kahā̃. These contracted kā̃/kã forms need a regional historical or morphological account before selecting one parent; no here-to-where edge is inferred from search.

### 8. Full CDIAL ka and kim belong to the interrogative paradigm but distinguish their reflexes: ka lists Romani kē/ke what, eastern ke and Kotgarhi kɛ, while kim lists Punjabi kī, Kalasha kīa and regional kyā. Bare survey ke/ke̤ in these NW and central/eastern lects does not alone select which historical inflection or remodeling underlies the response. A local paradigm or independently verified dictionary analysis is needed; uncertain cross-IA transmission alone is not the reason for holding.

### 14. The small/young component plausibly belongs to ślakṣṇa 12732; same-survey standalone nānlo/nānlu/nānko small supports segmentation, but the full article does not establish these -l/-k/-ḍḍ extensions. Other records also have -is/-iś/-iṣ on the kin term or a distinct dādo/bayi stem. Research the local morphology and exact kin term before a complete component analysis; the hold is not solely uncertainty about cross-IA borrowing.

### 172. Supported proposed branch CDIAL[5853.3] (5853-3), but that exact subsection node is absent from current compiled forms and no persistent alias was found. Validation rejected the batch before any overlay write. Preserve this analysis for a source/section repair; do not substitute the broad head or a remote donor. Evidence: Full CDIAL tuṇḍa subsection 3 tōṇḍa explicitly gives Prakrit toṃḍa mouth, Marathi tõḍ mouth and Konkani toṃḍa. The selected o-vowel toṇḍ/ṭõḍ/tonḍə/tõḍ-type survey mouth forms fit that specifically numbered branch. Initial t/ṭ, nasalization, final stop and vowel notation remain unchanged; Turner explicitly allows IA retroflex assimilation and dialectal or expressive variation across this family. Regional transmission is unresolved, and no remote Dravidian or Munda donor is substituted for the attested IA branch. Exact response: ṭõḍ.

### 354. Supported proposed branch CDIAL[5853.3] (5853-3), but that exact subsection node is absent from current compiled forms and no persistent alias was found. Validation rejected the batch before any overlay write. Preserve this analysis for a source/section repair; do not substitute the broad head or a remote donor. Evidence: Full CDIAL tuṇḍa subsection 3 tōṇḍa explicitly gives Prakrit toṃḍa mouth, Marathi tõḍ mouth and Konkani toṃḍa. The selected o-vowel toṇḍ/ṭõḍ/tonḍə/tõḍ-type survey mouth forms fit that specifically numbered branch. Initial t/ṭ, nasalization, final stop and vowel notation remain unchanged; Turner explicitly allows IA retroflex assimilation and dialectal or expressive variation across this family. Regional transmission is unresolved, and no remote Dravidian or Munda donor is substituted for the attested IA branch. Exact response: toṇḍ.

## Qualifications retained in saved analyses

The user explicitly prefers linking supported etyma when cross-Indo-Aryan borrowing is the unresolved issue. 345 previously held survey records have therefore been linked provisionally; their reflex edges identify the comparative family without claiming an established inheritance path or immediate donor. Every prior hold and transmission qualification is retained in [contact-decisions.json](/Users/aryamanarora/Documents/Code/jambu-all/data/curation/etymology-lab/indo-aryan-sil-20260914/contact-decisions.json).

Some saved parent families have uncertain deeper histories. Linking to a specific existing comparative family does not settle its remote origin. Examples include *ēkka (emphatic strengthening or learned replacement), *grilla (deeper derivation explicitly doubtful), vaḍra (extracted formation rather than simple vṛddha descent), and several expressive or possible substrate families. The evidence notes retain those qualifications.

## Resolved source-attribution issue

148 previously held Rana Tharu records now have saved analyses. The [Webster source audit](/Users/aryamanarora/Documents/Code/jambu-all/data/data/other/forms/raw_data/sil_western_tharu_2017/README.md) records zero ambiguous lexical readings: the uncertainty concerns mapping duplicate RNS codes to Sisaikhara versus Sisana, both Rana Tharu. Source tags and locality uncertainty are retained. Three re-examined records still have independent etymological holds. Exact resolutions are in [rana-resolution-decisions.json](/Users/aryamanarora/Documents/Code/jambu-all/data/curation/etymology-lab/indo-aryan-sil-20260914/rana-resolution-decisions.json).

## Additional transmission-policy resolutions

A further 125 previously held Dardic and northwestern numeral records were reviewed by family and saved under the same policy. Their original holds and qualifications are retained in [contact-dardic-decisions.json](/Users/aryamanarora/Documents/Code/jambu-all/data/curation/etymology-lab/indo-aryan-sil-20260914/contact-dardic-decisions.json). Star, cow and new-adjective branch ambiguities were not waived.

## Corrected primary-source page links

A check against the cached CDIAL page array found 25 off-by-one page URLs in the seventh and ninth research passes. All were corrected; the other 203 checked article locators already matched. Exact entry/section citations and saved lexical rows were unaffected. See [primary-page-audit.json](/Users/aryamanarora/Documents/Code/jambu-all/data/curation/etymology-lab/indo-aryan-sil-20260914/primary-page-audit.json).

## Resolved initial h correspondences

22 initial-h records were resolved using at least two other s/ś > h comparison families from each exact survey locality (including h-initial hundred within ek hau). The evidence matrix is preserved in [local-h-matrix.json](/Users/aryamanarora/Documents/Code/jambu-all/data/curation/etymology-lab/indo-aryan-sil-20260914/local-h-matrix.json). This supports the local correspondence without importing another survey locality’s sound history.

## Separate validation issue

The focused assignment suite reported 16 passes and one failure: the self-reference regression test expects 2,604 rows, while both the pre-save backup and final overlay contain 2,603 matching self-reference rows. This count mismatch predates the new assignments. It was not fixed as part of this etymological review. See [test evidence](/Users/aryamanarora/Documents/Code/jambu-all/data/curation/etymology-lab/indo-aryan-sil-20260914/test-results.json).

## Western proximal correction

Four a/ā “this” assignments were withdrawn after checking Chatterji 1926, Part II, pp. 832–833: Gujarati ā continues ayam, whereas the Rajasthani paradigm includes eta-family forms. This is a competing-root issue. The four exact records, withdrawn rows and fresh validation are preserved in [proximal-correction.json](/Users/aryamanarora/Documents/Code/jambu-all/data/curation/etymology-lab/indo-aryan-sil-20260914/proximal-correction.json); primary pages are in [chatterji-pronoun-primary.json](/Users/aryamanarora/Documents/Code/jambu-all/data/curation/etymology-lab/indo-aryan-sil-20260914/chatterji-pronoun-primary.json).

## Provisional regional loan routes

Saved borrowed edges to existing Hindi attestations identify a supported regional lexical donor hypothesis. The attestation locality is not asserted to be the historical donor locality. Intermediate Indo-Aryan transmission remains open; immediate Hindi/Urdu routes are distinguished from remote Persian or Arabic origin in each evidence note.

## User-resolved branches

The user selected sauvarṇa for the 114 indistinguishable gold cases and the Gujarati ayam analysis for the three Bhil-language a/ā proximal demonstratives. These 117 links are saved with the scholarly uncertainty retained as an editorial qualification. See [user-resolution-decisions.json](/Users/aryamanarora/Documents/Code/jambu-all/data/curation/etymology-lab/indo-aryan-sil-20260914/user-resolution-decisions.json) and [editorial-preferences.json](/Users/aryamanarora/Documents/Code/jambu-all/data/curation/etymology-lab/indo-aryan-sil-20260914/editorial-preferences.json).

## Suspected twenty/hundred source alignment

Pass 84 flags 21 northern numeral records: five-score expressions occur under twenty (item 163), while bare bīś occurs under hundred (item 164). CDIAL 11616 identifies bīś as twenty. Read-only investigation verifies that all 21 rows used the importer’s first-column token heuristic. A boundary shift is now the supported hypothesis; intended cells still await PDF-layout confirmation. See [parser evidence](/Users/aryamanarora/Documents/Code/jambu-all/data/curation/etymology-lab/indo-aryan-sil-20260914/numeral-boundary-investigation-20260915.md). See [exact source locations](/Users/aryamanarora/Documents/Code/jambu-all/data/curation/etymology-lab/indo-aryan-sil-20260914/pass84-decisions.json).

## Sibling expression components

Passes 90 and 91 save 93 complete sibling expressions as ordered adjective + kinship components: small/young + brother/sister, or big/elder + brother/sister. The links name the component etymological families and do not assert that the whole phrases are inherited Sanskrit compounds. Ambiguous concatenations and unmatched second words remain for source or lexical review. See [younger siblings](/Users/aryamanarora/Documents/Code/jambu-all/data/curation/etymology-lab/indo-aryan-sil-20260914/pass90-decisions.json) and [older siblings](/Users/aryamanarora/Documents/Code/jambu-all/data/curation/etymology-lab/indo-aryan-sil-20260914/pass91-decisions.json).

## Numeral phrase components

Pass 83 links 33 complete hundred expressions through two ordered etymological components: one × hundred or five × score. These point to the documented component families, not to a supposed inherited whole Sanskrit phrase. Nine additional simple hundred records take ordinary śata links. Exact forms, scope and evidence are in [pass 83 decisions](/Users/aryamanarora/Documents/Code/jambu-all/data/curation/etymology-lab/indo-aryan-sil-20260914/pass83-decisions.json).

## Compound and inherited-branch qualifications

Groundnut: eight Hindi mūṅg-phalī records now have ordered mung + pod component edges; 148 regional whole-word forms point to the attested Hindi compound. Platts distinguishes this lexicalized groundnut name from mūṅg kī phalī, an actual mung pod. See [compound plan](/Users/aryamanarora/Documents/Code/jambu-all/data/curation/etymology-lab/indo-aryan-sil-20260914/groundnut-compound-plan.json).

Noon: new do-/du-pahar links point to the whole Hindi word. Its older *dva-prahara ancestry differs from Platts’s dvi-prahara; the older edge was preserved and the discrepancy is retained in [noon ancestry audit](/Users/aryamanarora/Documents/Code/jambu-all/data/curation/etymology-lab/indo-aryan-sil-20260914/noon-upstream-audit.json).

Western vāṭ path follows Turner’s placement under vartman, with the explicitly acknowledged vartis alternative retained. Śītala-family cold forms retain Turner’s *śītalla / *śaitalya-crossing qualification and unexplained d in northern śidāl forms. Sikatā-family sand forms retain possible Iranian contact and suffix variation. Exact evidence is in [pass 41](/Users/aryamanarora/Documents/Code/jambu-all/data/curation/etymology-lab/indo-aryan-sil-20260914/global-seventh-decisions.json) and [pass 42](/Users/aryamanarora/Documents/Code/jambu-all/data/curation/etymology-lab/indo-aryan-sil-20260914/global-eighth-decisions.json).

## Mother and adjacent kinship cells

The cached Hindko source has adjacent columns 106 mother, 107 older brother and 108 younger brother. Some compiled mother responses include mā followed by vada pirā, matching the adjacent older-brother cell. These records were held for source-layout review, without changing source forms or treating the concatenation as a lexeme. Related Gujari responses require the same check. See [exact examined records](/Users/aryamanarora/Documents/Code/jambu-all/data/curation/etymology-lab/indo-aryan-sil-20260914/week-mother-decisions.json) and the [cached source](/Users/aryamanarora/Documents/Code/jambu-all/data/data/other/forms/raw_data/hindko).

## Further branch, sound and source checks

Khowar sorūm gold is saved as an early Sanskrit loan under the user-selected sauvarṇa branch, with final m analogically drawn from silver. See [Khowar gold decisions](/Users/aryamanarora/Documents/Code/jambu-all/data/curation/etymology-lab/indo-aryan-sil-20260914/gold-khowar-decisions.json).

Khandesi miṭh salt is linked with mṛṣṭa/miṣṭa sweet: Turner explicitly records salt in Marathi and Konkani. Ash rakhoḍo/rakhoḍi selects *rakṣāpuṭaka rather than simple rakṣā. Bhil tātlo/tātlũ hot selects the ll-extension *taptall-. See [pass 50](/Users/aryamanarora/Documents/Code/jambu-all/data/curation/etymology-lab/indo-aryan-sil-20260914/global-eleventh-decisions.json) and [temperature analysis](/Users/aryamanarora/Documents/Code/jambu-all/data/curation/etymology-lab/indo-aryan-sil-20260914/thermal-decisions.json).

Held discoveries include ant mistakenly suggested from pinch, when from count, and Khowar/Gawri sit forms whose full article specifies Nuristani borrowing. These require corrected families or immediate donors, not a waiver of cross-Indo-Aryan uncertainty.

Forty-three source responses consist only of dashes or no entry. They are examined source-data holds, not unknown etymologies of attested words; no lexical assignment was fabricated. See [missing-response cells](/Users/aryamanarora/Documents/Code/jambu-all/data/curation/etymology-lab/indo-aryan-sil-20260914/missing-response-decisions.json).

Forty-two slash-separated responses were linked only after every alternative independently matched an already reviewed attestation in the same language and meaning, with one shared parent and relation kind. The source cells remain intact; no compound-component relation is inferred from a slash. See [per-alternative evidence](/Users/aryamanarora/Documents/Code/jambu-all/data/curation/etymology-lab/indo-aryan-sil-20260914/alternant-candidates.json).

## Hajong initial-h resolution

Twenty-five Hajong records, including sixteen earlier holds, were resolved jointly using snake, pig, thread and dry comparisons from the same exact survey localities. At least two other lexical families support each target’s working initial h correspondence to regional s/ś/x. The analysis does not claim that local inheritance versus borrowing is settled, or assign opaque suffixes merely because the initial consonant is explained. See [exact-locality matrix](/Users/aryamanarora/Documents/Code/jambu-all/data/curation/etymology-lab/indo-aryan-sil-20260914/hajong-h-matrix.json) and [saved decisions](/Users/aryamanarora/Documents/Code/jambu-all/data/curation/etymology-lab/indo-aryan-sil-20260914/hajong-h-decisions.json).

## Western child-family comparison

The por-/pur- child terms are linked under CDIAL 8399.2 *potara, anchored by Gujarati porī/poriyo and Marathi por. Vowel raising and glide position in pury-, poir- and poyar- remain working comparative analyses, not independently established sound laws. See [comparative audit](/Users/aryamanarora/Documents/Code/jambu-all/data/curation/etymology-lab/indo-aryan-sil-20260914/child-r-comparative-audit.md) and [exact locality series](/Users/aryamanarora/Documents/Code/jambu-all/data/curation/etymology-lab/indo-aryan-sil-20260914/child-r-locality-series.json).

## Priority source error: dental stops in Noira 2015

Visual checks show printed dental d̪/t̪ stored as retroflex ɖ/ʈ in the hand-keyed ledger. Links now follow the verified printed readings while stored forms and IDs remain unchanged. A global replacement would be unsafe because genuine retroflex stops also occur. See [source-error audit](/Users/aryamanarora/Documents/Code/jambu-all/data/curation/etymology-lab/indo-aryan-sil-20260914/noira-dental-source-audit.md) and [exact verified cells](/Users/aryamanarora/Documents/Code/jambu-all/data/curation/etymology-lab/indo-aryan-sil-20260914/noira-dental-verified-cells.json).

## Pass 127: hearing verbs

Saved 46 simple hearing responses under CDIAL 12598, with verbal endings preserved and regional s/h, ś/ç, dental/retroflex and vowel variation qualified in every decision. The source explicitly supplies eastern sunab/sunal and Assamese xuniba as well as Jaunsari śūṇnõ. Homboḷ-/somḷ- forms, auxiliary/light-verb responses, and mixed gold/hear records remain for separate analysis; no source spelling was changed.

## Pass 128: western hearing family

Saved 19 western hearing responses under CDIAL 12962 *saṁbhalati, using its explicit Prakrit, Old Gujarati and Gujarati hearing attestations. Full 12961 support/take-care and 13057 remember entries were compared. Initial s/h, mb reduction and contracted homl-/homel- shapes are qualified in the decisions; the fuller hombal-/homboḷ- regional series anchors these comparisons. The le-/ledu responses and homilinu remain for separate morphological analysis. This resolves part of the pass-127 western-family research queue.

## Pass 129: sleep stems

Saved 84 simple sleep responses across svápati 13902, suptá 13479 and *ghummati 4485. The latter explicitly includes eastern doze/sleep senses and the Bengali verbal form in the addenda. Inflection, western s/h and l-extensions, and Tharu dental/retroflex notation are qualified in the saved decisions. Ten Awan/Pothwari se responses are held: short se does not securely distinguish svápati from śayate/śete histories. Auxiliary and negative constructions remain separately unresearched.

## Pass 130: giving verbs

Saved 45 simple giving responses under dadāti 6141, the specific *dita branch 6140-3, arpayati 684 and ālīyate 1387. These last two have explicit Gujarati give/pay attestations. Seven Hajong di/diva responses are held because Assamese diba appears under both 6141 and the dīyate 6365 addenda; the local forms do not select a history. Past api-/apyo forms and compound de-de responses remain for morphology and the independently attested arpita branch. Full primary articles and exact decisions are preserved.

## Pass 131: coming verbs

Saved 67 simple coming responses under āpayati 1200, āgata 1045 and āviśati 1437, distinguishing present stems, old past stems and the eastern āisā family. Regional contractions and s/h realizations are qualified in the decisions. Eleven Pothwari aś/as/ac responses are held because CDIAL 227 and 1044 explicitly discuss competing atyeti/āgacchati derivations. Western aviyu/auiyu/aviyo past forms remain for analogical or productive present-stem morphology rather than being assigned mechanically to āgata; paired present/past responses also need separate analysis.

## Pass 132: descending verbs

Saved Kathoriya gir and Kochila Tharu girale under *girati 4159 and Dang uṭər under uttarati 1770, preserving the directional gloss and retroflex notation. Sixteen simple jhar-/jar- descending responses are held: 5328 *jhaṭati explicitly allows 5346 *jharati for Nepali jharnu, so the comparison does not select a unique root. Negative/auxiliary responses, short uṭ, and other unrelated descending stems remain for separate work.

## Pass 133: drinking and consuming

Saved 32 simple drinking responses under pibati 8209, khādati 3865 and the khādita past branch 3865-2. Fresh Platts page 868 explicitly supports drink as a meaning of khānā; the full public response and URL are preserved in pass133-platts-research.json. Regional deaspiration and l-endings are qualified. Water-plus-verb compounds, light-verb forms, the pid-/piḍ- series, and duk-/kam- responses remain for separate work; no claim is made that every drink response comes from pibati.

## Pass 134: eating verbs

Saved 47 simple eating responses, separating khādati 3865 from khādita 3865-2 and its explicitly documented analogical khādhũ/khādhā past forms. Dental/retroflex transcription and western l-extensions are qualified in the decisions. Hadothi jeo is linked to jemati 5267, and Marwari jīmṇa to *jimyati/*jimmati 5267-3; the primary entry records a disputed deeper Munda connection, which these links do not settle. Compound eating responses and the short jim- branch remain for separate work.

## Pass 135: Dardic eating stems

Saved ten Khowar, Kalasha, Gawri and Dameli eating responses under 10507 yuvate, citing the explicit eat sense in subsection 2. The canonical node has no separate subsection-2 node and displays yokes, so the full primary prose is essential. Turner tentatively derives Khowar žib- from *yuvvati with expressive doubling; that qualification, verbal endings and source spacing are retained in every decision. The distinct ravate grain-eating family 10645 was compared.
